Until now the thread carried no listing link (the M2 opener text was the only clue); now both buyer and seller see a listing card pinned to the thread, and the conversation holds a machine-readable pointer — the seam a checkout/order will hang off. No new AWS infra (the card is a JSON attribute on the existing conversation meta item).

**The module seam — a generic context card.** New public [`messaging.ConversationContext`](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/messaging/ConversationContext.java) `(kind, refId, title, subtitle, imageUrl)` — deliberately vertical-agnostic (a `delivery` order or `travel` ride would carry the same shape), so `messaging` never gains a dependency on `economy`. It's **opaque to messaging**: `kind`/`refId` let the client (and later payments) resolve the real object; title/subtitle/imageUrl are the pre-rendered card so drawing it needs no cross-module read. Stored as `contextJson` on the `CONV#{cid}/META` item; echoed on `ConversationDto`. `MessagingApi` gained an overload `openDirectConversation(caller, other, context)` (the old 2-arg form delegates with `null`); `ModularityTests` confirms `economy → messaging` is still public-API-only.

**Refresh-on-reuse.** The buyer↔seller 1:1 is deduped by pair (M2), so a buyer who messages the same seller about a *second* listing reuses the thread — and `createConversation` now **overwrites the card** (`repo.updateContext`) to the listing being asked about now. Both sides see the update on their next fetch/poll. (Trade-off: one thread per pair means the card reflects the most-recent listing, not a per-listing thread; matches the existing "one My World 1:1 per person" model and is what a single order/checkout wants to attach to.)

**Economy** — [`ListingService.openChat`](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/economy/internal/ListingService.java) builds the card from the listing: `subtitle` = the grouped price label or `"On ask"` for a null price, `imageUrl` = the first listing image. `POST /v1/economy/listings/{id}/chat` is otherwise unchanged (still returns `{conversationId, sellerId, suggestedMessage}` and posts nothing).

**iOS** — `ConversationContextDTO` + `ConversationDTO.context`; UI model `WorldListingContext` (parses `refId`→`listingID` when `kind=="listing"`) on `WorldConversation`, mapped in `MessagingStore`. [WorldChatView](GojoGo/Screens/WorldChatView.swift) renders an opaque, tappable card pinned under the chat header (thumbnail + title + price + "View ›"); the thread's top inset grows so messages clear it. Tapping → `AppState.openListingContext` fetches the listing (`EconomyStore.get`, since it may not be in the loaded catalog page), seeds the catalog so `ProductDetailView` resolves it, and opens the detail — with a card-derived fallback product if the fetch fails, so the tap is never dead. The field-by-field conversation merge leaves the card server-authoritative (the incoming row carries it).

**Verified (2026-07-24)** — `ModularityTests` green (Corretto 21), iOS `xcodebuild -sdk iphonesimulator` → BUILD SUCCEEDED, and a prod two-user curl E2E (`verify_listing_context.sh`, session-local): A lists a priced Leica + an "on ask" lens → B opens chat on the Leica → **B's** conversation shows `context {kind:listing, refId:<leica>, title, subtitle:"12,000 MAD"}` → **A (seller)** sees the identical card → B opens chat on the "on ask" lens → **same thread** (`conversationId` unchanged) with the card **refreshed** to `refId:<lens>, subtitle:"On ask"`. Test listings cleaned up (204s).

**Not covered:** no listing card yet on the *conversation list* row (only inside the open thread); a live socket push of a context change on reuse (the seller learns the refreshed card on next fetch/poll, not instantly); and the SwiftUI card is exercised by build + curl, not a two-identity simulator run (same pending on-device pass as the rest of 2b).

## Phase 2b · Milestone 4 — delivery vertical (deployed + verified 2026-07-25)

The other half of commerce (ARCHITECTURE.md §10 Phase 2b). GojoDelivery was the last vertical wired to an **empty** `SampleData` catalog — the tab showed its empty state — so this milestone is the whole loop: browse → menu → cart → order → track → rate → order again. No new AWS infra (reuses RDS; `V8__delivery.sql` runs on startup and creates the `delivery` schema).

**Backend — `com.gojogo.delivery` module** (new): entities `Merchant` / `MenuSection` / `MenuItem` / `CustomerOrder` / `OrderLine`; `DeliveryService`, `DeliveryController`, `DeliveryTimeline`, `OrderFulfilmentJob`, and `DeliveryCurrentProfile` (uniquely named per the bean-collision incident). Publishes public `delivery.OrderPlaced` and `delivery.OrderStatusChanged` — no consumer yet, mirroring `economy.ListingCreated`; `OrderStatusChanged` is the hook an "your food is on the way" APNs push hangs off (`notifications` already consumes `messaging.MessageSent` that way).

Decisions worth keeping straight:

- **The server prices the order.** The request carries `{menuItemId, qty}` only; names and unit prices are read from the menu, copied onto the order lines (so a re-priced dish never rewrites an old receipt), and the delivery fee comes from the merchant row. The client's cart total is a preview — `DeliveryRestaurant` gained `feeCents` so that preview matches instead of the old hardcoded `$1.49`.
- **Cart stays on the device.** A per-tap cart sync buys nothing here and costs a request per `+`; the order is the transaction, and it's whole-cart.
- **One live order per user** — a second `POST` while one is in flight is a `409`, matching a UI that tracks exactly one order.
- **Fulfilment is simulated, but the state machine is real.** Offsets from `placedAt` (config `gojogo.delivery.timeline.*`, default 20s → 75s → 150s → 330s) say where an order *should* be; `OrderFulfilmentJob` (every 10s) walks each open order there and publishes every step it passes through. Because state is recomputed from `placedAt` rather than accumulated, a missed tick, a restart or a slow deploy catches up instead of stranding an order in "Preparing". `CustomerOrder` carries a `@Version` so two backend instances can't both advance the same order. When `dispatch` lands (Phase 3), this job is the only thing it replaces.
- **Cancel window**: allowed through `COURIER_TO_RESTAURANT`, `409` once the food is moving (`DELIVERING`) — the same rule the UI's cancel button already assumed.

Schema (`delivery` schema, no cross-schema FKs — `user_id` is a plain UUID):

| Table | Columns |
|---|---|
| `delivery.merchant` | id, name, cuisine, rating, review_count, eta_minutes, delivery_fee_cents, image_url, promo, latitude, longitude, active, created_at |
| `delivery.merchant_category` / `merchant_tag` | (merchant_id, category) / (merchant_id, tag) — categories are the browse filter, tags are display chips |
| `delivery.menu_section` · `delivery.menu_item` | section: merchant_id, name, sort_order · item: section_id, name, detail, price_cents, image_url, popular, available, sort_order |
| `delivery.customer_order` | id, user_id, merchant_id, status, subtotal/delivery_fee/service_fee/total cents, currency, address_label, note, courier_*, rating, placed_at, status_changed_at, eta_at, closed_at, version |
| `delivery.order_line` | order_id, menu_item_id, name, unit_price_cents, qty, sort_order |

**Seeded catalog** — 6 Casablanca restaurants (Atlas Burger Co., Forno Nero, Kaido Sushi, El Tapeo, Verde Bowl, Maison Sucrée), 12 menu sections, 25 items, covering the category strip the app already shipped. Fixed UUIDs, so a rebuilt database keeps the same item ids. Merchant self-onboarding belongs to `partner` in a later phase; images are null for now and render as the app's placeholder.

**API surface** (all Bearer-authed):
- `GET /v1/delivery/merchants?category=&q=&limit=` — active restaurants, fastest first; menus omitted
- `GET /v1/delivery/merchants/{id}` — the only response carrying the menu
- `POST /v1/delivery/orders` `{merchantId, lines[{menuItemId, qty}], addressLabel, note}` → 201 (409 if one is live, 400 on an item that isn't on that menu)
- `GET /v1/delivery/orders/active` → `{order}` (null when nothing is in flight — a 404 would be noise on a poll) · `GET /v1/delivery/orders?limit=` (history) · `GET /v1/delivery/orders/{id}`
- `POST /v1/delivery/orders/{id}/cancel` · `POST /v1/delivery/orders/{id}/rate` `{rating}` (1–5, delivered only)

**iOS** — `DeliveryModels` (DTOs) + `DeliveryStore` (REST + DTO→`DeliveryRestaurant`/`DeliveryMenuItem`/`DeliveryCourier` mapping, `remoteMerchantIds` gate) + [AppState+Delivery.swift](GojoGo/Models/AppState+Delivery.swift). `connectBackend` → `refreshDelivery()` swaps in the live catalog, re-attaches to an order already in flight, and loads history into the "Order again" rail. Opening a restaurant lazily fetches its menu. Checkout routes to `placeLiveDeliveryOrder` for a server-backed restaurant (optimistic tracking screen; a failed call hands the cart back), and the tracking screen then **mirrors the backend** — status, ETA, courier, and the moving-pin progress all come from the poll (every 5s, by order id so a delivered order stays up for its rating) instead of the on-device timer. Cancel and the star rating post to the API; a `409` on a too-late cancel puts the tracking screen back rather than pretending. SampleData restaurants (i.e. offline) keep the original local simulation untouched.

**Verified (2026-07-25)** — backend `ModularityTests` green (Corretto 21); iOS `xcodebuild -sdk iphonesimulator` → BUILD SUCCEEDED; V8 applied on the Fargate roll and the context started clean (so Hibernate `validate` accepted the new mappings). **Prod two-user curl E2E green, 52/52** (`verify_delivery.sh`, session-local): catalog count/order/fee, category + text filters, menu-only-on-detail, unknown restaurant 404 → A orders (subtotal `2×890+390`, fee 149, service 99, total **2418** — all server-computed), second order 409, B can't read A's order (404), cross-restaurant item 400, empty cart 400 → A's order reaches PREPARING, cancels (200), cancel again 409, rating a cancelled order 409 → B rides an order through COURIER_TO_RESTAURANT (courier assigned) and DELIVERING (progress climbing 0.04→1.0, ETA counting 6→1) to DELIVERED, with a cancel attempt during DELIVERING correctly **409**, rate-before-delivery 409, rating 9 → 400, rating 5 → 200 → history holds both users' orders, receipts and note intact, and neither sees the other's.

**Also verified in the simulator on the live backend** (the on-device pass the earlier 2b milestones never got): signed in as test user A → GojoDelivery showed the **live** catalog (Verde Bowl 4.5 · 17 min · *Delivery free*, El Tapeo's "20% off" promo) → opening Verde Bowl lazily loaded its menu (Bowls/Juices, `$11.40`/`$12.90`/`$6.20`, "Popular" badge, Gluten free / Vegan tags) → adding items drove the cart bar and a checkout whose fee lines matched the merchant (Free delivery, $0.99 service, **$12.39**) → "Place order" created the order in prod (curl confirmed `Verde Bowl`, `1239`, address label `Home · 12 Rue Atlas`) → the tracking screen showed the **server's** 6-minute ETA (not the catalog's 17) and, ~90s later, advanced by itself to "Courier picking up · 4 min" with courier **Amine T. ★4.91 · On foot** from the backend roster → "Cancel order" returned to browse and the server showed the order `CANCELLED` with nothing active. A second order (2× bowl, `$23.79`) was then ridden all the way through in the UI: the pin walked the route, the cancel button disappeared at "On the way", "Delivered" showed with the star prompt, and 5 stars + Done wrote `rating: 5` to the order and put it in the **"Order again"** rail (`6m · $23.79`) — with the two cancelled orders correctly absent from that rail.

**Not covered / deferred:** no per-item options/extras; no live courier geo (the map animates the pin along a synthetic route — real positions come with Phase 3 `dispatch`); and `OrderPlaced` / `OrderStatusChanged` have no consumer yet (order push is the obvious next slice). The two gaps this section originally listed — no error surface, and a hardcoded delivery address — are closed in the follow-up below.

### Follow-up (2026-07-25): demo catalog dropped, real addresses, error surface

Three changes on top of M4, deployed together as `V9__delivery_addresses.sql`.

**1. The seeded demo catalog is gone.** V8's six restaurants were scaffolding to prove the loop; they're deleted (along with the E2E orders that referenced them — `customer_order` has a plain FK to `merchant`, so those had to go first). V8 is deliberately left untouched: it has already run in prod, and rewriting an applied migration breaks Flyway's checksum, so a fresh database inserts the seed and V9 removes it. **GojoDelivery now shows its empty state until real merchants exist** — a merchant-onboarding surface (the `partner` vertical) is what unblocks it.

**2. Delivery addresses are real.** New `delivery.address` table (label, street, courier note, optional coordinates, `is_default`) with **one default per user enforced by a partial unique index**, not by application hope. The order copies the address onto itself at order time — same rule as line prices, so editing or deleting a saved address never rewrites an old receipt (`customer_order` gained `address_id`, `address_line`, `address_note`, and coordinates; there is deliberately no FK back to `address`).
- `GET /v1/delivery/addresses` · `POST /v1/delivery/addresses` · `PUT /v1/delivery/addresses/{id}` · `POST /v1/delivery/addresses/{id}/default` · `DELETE /v1/delivery/addresses/{id}`
- `POST /v1/delivery/orders` takes `addressId`; omitting it uses the caller's default, and an order with **no** address is a `400` ("Add a delivery address first"). The old free-text `addressLabel` is still accepted so an older client keeps working.
- Rules that make the UI simple: the first address a user saves becomes their default automatically, and deleting the default promotes the next one — a user with addresses left always has one selected.
- **iOS** — [DeliveryAddressSheet](GojoGo/Screens/DeliveryAddressSheet.swift): saved addresses as tinted-glass rows (`.glass(tint:)`, the app's Liquid-Glass modifier) with a house/office/pin icon, DEFAULT chip, checkmark on the selected one, and an ellipsis menu for edit/delete; an inline add/edit form with Home/Work/Other label chips, street + courier-note fields, and **"Use my current location"** (one-shot GPS + reverse geocode, reusing the provider behind chat location pins). Opens from the browse header's "Deliver to" row and from checkout — checkout presents its own copy so it stacks over the sheet instead of fighting the root view's sheet slot, and the place-order button reads "Add a delivery address" (and opens the sheet) when there's nowhere to deliver.

**3. GojoDelivery can admit failure.** A tinted-glass notice banner (top of the section, auto-dismisses, tap-to-close) now surfaces: a failed order placement (the cart is handed back and *says so*), a refused cancel, and address save/delete failures — preferring **the backend's own message** ("Too late to cancel — your courier is on the way") over a generic one. A failed save also re-reads the address list, so a screen that just proved itself stale reconciles instead of lingering.

**Verified (2026-07-25)** — `ModularityTests` green, iOS `xcodebuild` → BUILD SUCCEEDED, V9 applied on the Fargate roll with a clean startup (Hibernate `validate` accepted the new table and columns). **Prod two-user curl E2E green, 33/33** (`verify_addresses.sh`, session-local): catalog is empty and a seeded restaurant id is now `404`, its orders are gone → create/edit/delete addresses, first-is-default, `makeDefault` switching (always exactly one default, listed first), delete-the-default promotes the survivor, blank street `400`, and B can neither read, edit, nor delete A's addresses (`404` each). **Simulator pass on the live backend:** the delivery tab shows "No restaurants yet"; the header reads "Add a delivery address"; adding one through the sheet wrote it to prod (curl-confirmed) and the header updated; the saved row rendered with its DEFAULT chip and checkmark; deleting from the ellipsis menu emptied it server-side. The notice banner was exercised for real — an address deleted behind the app's back made "Save" fail, and the banner showed the backend's own **"No such address"** while the list reconciled itself.

**Known limits of this follow-up:** with an empty catalog there is no way to place an order in prod, so the *order-carries-address* path (address copied onto the receipt, missing-address `400`) is compile- and review-verified only — it will be exercised the first time a real merchant exists. The address form is free text (no autocomplete/map pin beyond "use my location"), and the notice banner floats over the "Deliver to" row while it's up.

### Deploy runbook (executed 2026-07-25; the backend now runs on ECS/Fargate, **not** App Runner)

**Superseded 2026-07-25 by [scripts/deploy-backend.sh](scripts/deploy-backend.sh)** — see "Deploying the backend" below. The hand-rolled version this milestone used was:

```
export JAVA_HOME=/Users/mac/Library/Java/JavaVirtualMachines/corretto-21.0.5/Contents/Home
cd backend && mvn -B -DskipTests compile jib:build \
  -Djib.image=578109959809.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/gojogo-backend:latest \
  -Djib.to.auth.username=AWS -Djib.to.auth.password="$(aws ecr get-login-password --region us-east-1)"
aws ecs update-service --cluster gojogo --service gojogo-backend --force-new-deployment --region us-east-1
```

(Older milestone runbooks in this file end with `aws apprunner start-deployment` — that service is retired.)

## Deploying the backend (2026-07-25)

Both paths run the same script, so CI and a laptop deploy can't drift.

**Automatic** — any push to `main` touching `backend/**` deploys it ([.github/workflows/deploy-backend.yml](.github/workflows/deploy-backend.yml), concurrency-limited to one at a time). The workflow previously ended with `apprunner start-deployment` against the service the Fargate migration destroyed, so pushes built an image and then silently failed to deploy it; it now just calls the script.

**Manual** — `./scripts/deploy-backend.sh`. Preflight → `mvn test` → Jib push → new task definition revision → rollout wait → health check, stopping at the first failure.

**⚠️ Blocked on an IAM change (2026-07-25) — needs an admin.** Registering a task definition hands ECS the task + execution roles, so it needs `iam:PassRole` with `iam:PassedToService` including `ecs-tasks.amazonaws.com`. The old deploy path (`force-new-deployment` on a mutable tag) never passed a role, so this was never needed. The first CI run failed exactly there — tests green, image `e0e1ad6` pushed, then `AccessDeniedException ... iam:PassRole`.

`iam-policy-milestone1.json` now carries the fix. Two traps found while preparing it, both fixed in the file: it had drifted **behind** the applied policy (it was missing the live `CloudFrontForMediaCdn` and `S3ForUserMediaBuckets` statements, so applying it verbatim would have *revoked* media permissions), and it used a literal `ACCOUNT_ID` placeholder that the documented `create-policy-version` command would have installed as-is. The file is now a faithful mirror of the applied **v9** plus `ecs.amazonaws.com` / `ecs-tasks.amazonaws.com`, with real account ids. Apply as admin:

```bash
aws iam create-policy-version \
  --policy-arn arn:aws:iam::578109959809:policy/GojoGoMilestone1Policy \
  --policy-document file://iam-policy-milestone1.json --set-as-default
```

(On the 5-version limit: `aws iam delete-policy-version --policy-arn <arn> --version-id vN` for the oldest non-default.) Then re-run the deploy — it reuses the image already in ECR rather than rebuilding.

**Images are pinned by immutable tag, and that's the point.** The service used to run `:latest`, which every deploy overwrote — so "roll back to the previous task definition" got you the same broken image, and rollback was effectively manual archaeology. Each deploy now pushes `<git-sha>` and registers a task definition revision pinned to it (`latest` still gets pushed, but nothing runs from it). Revision N-1 therefore still references exactly what it shipped:

```
./scripts/deploy-backend.sh --list       # revisions, their images, which is live
./scripts/deploy-backend.sh --rollback   # back one revision, no rebuild
```

Consequences worth knowing:

- **A clean tree is required.** A dirty tree would tag an image with a commit that doesn't contain the code, which is exactly what breaks rollback later. `--allow-dirty` overrides and tags `<sha>-dirty-<timestamp>` so it can never be mistaken for the commit.
- **Re-deploying an existing tag is refused** — moving a tag would silently change what a deployed revision means. Commit and deploy the new SHA, or `--rollback` to the revision already running it.
- **`cdk deploy GojoGoFargateStack` re-asserts the task definition** and would knock the service back to a mutable tag. `imageTag` is now a required prop (context-driven, defaulting to `latest` for a cold bootstrap), so pass the running one:
  ```
  cdk deploy GojoGoFargateStack -c imageTag=$(./scripts/deploy-backend.sh --current) \
    -c domainName=api.gojogo.app -c certificateArn=...
  ```
- **A failed deploy is not an outage.** `desiredCount 1` / `minHealthyPercent 100` means the new task must go healthy before the old drains, so a bad build (or a failed Flyway migration, which looks identical) stalls the rollout while the previous version keeps serving. The script times out at 10 minutes and prints the two commands that diagnose it, plus the rollback.

## Phase 2b · Milestone 5 — seller listing management (deployed; prod-E2E-verified 2026-07-30)

Selling was one-way: publish and hope. A seller could create a listing and delete it, and nothing else — no edit, no way to say "sold", and no sight of whether anyone was interested. This milestone is the seller's side of the marketplace: **"Your listings"**, reached from a `Selling N` chip in the Economy chrome (and from "Manage listing" on your own listing detail).

**The status model is the core of it.** `active BOOLEAN` couldn't express this: *paused* and *sold* are both "not in browse" but mean opposite things to the seller, and to a buyer who saved the item. So `V10__economy_listing_management.sql` adds `status VARCHAR(16)` (`ACTIVE` / `PAUSED` / `SOLD`, CHECK-constrained), `view_count`, and `updated_at`, backfilling status from `active`. **`active` is deliberately left in place**, unread by the new code — dropping it would break the tasks still serving traffic mid-roll, whose entity maps the column. It keeps `NOT NULL DEFAULT true`, so inserts from either version succeed; a later migration can drop it once nothing maps it.

Decisions worth keeping straight:

- **Browse filters on status, everywhere.** The repository queries switched from `active = true` to `status = ACTIVE`, and `EconomyView.catalog` filters client-side too. That second filter is what makes it safe to *seed* a paused or sold listing into the catalog — which `openListingContext` (a thread's listing card) and "View in marketplace" both have to do, because `ProductDetailView` resolves through `liveProduct(id:)`.
- **Editing is a full replacement.** `PUT` posts every field back, photos included, so a cleared description means cleared. Status is *not* in the edit form — pause/relist/mark-sold are row actions, which keeps a relist one tap instead of a form round-trip.
- **Views count buyers, not the seller.** `GET /listings/{id}` bumps `view_count` only when the viewer isn't the owner. The response carries the pre-bump number; the shelf re-reads its own via `/mine`.
- **Totals come from the server.** `/mine/stats` aggregates across *every* listing, so the tiles aren't limited to the page in hand. A stats failure can't take the shelf down — the tiles fall back to counting what loaded.
- **Status changes move optimistically and revert.** The segment a row lives in is the whole point of the tap, so it moves immediately; a refused call puts the row and the catalog back *and says so*.

**API surface** (all Bearer-authed, owner-only on the mutations):
- `PUT /v1/economy/listings/{id}` — full replacement → the updated listing (403 non-owner, 404 gone)
- `PUT /v1/economy/listings/{id}/status` `{status}` — ACTIVE / PAUSED / SOLD (400 on an unknown value)
- `GET /v1/economy/listings/mine/stats` → `{total, active, paused, sold, saves, views}`
- `GET /v1/economy/listings/mine` — unchanged, but now returns paused and sold listings too (it's the only place they appear), and every `ListingResponse` gained `status` / `viewCount` / `updatedAt`

**iOS** — new [SellerListingsView.swift](GojoGo/Screens/SellerListingsView.swift): stat tiles (live / saves / views / sold), status segments with counts, and a row per listing carrying price, a status pill, saves + views, a one-tap primary action (Mark sold, or Relist when it's down), Edit, and an ellipsis menu (edit · view in marketplace · every other status · delete). Delete is alert-confirmed and says what makes it different from "sold". The edit form is the same sheet, swapped in with a transition — the pattern `DeliveryAddressSheet` already uses — with a multi-photo strip (upload to S3 first, cover badge on the first), title, price, category, condition, pickup area, and details. New `SellerListing` view model + `ListingStatus` enum in [EconomyModels.swift](GojoGo/Stores/EconomyModels.swift); shelf/edit/status wiring in [AppState+Economy.swift](GojoGo/Models/AppState+Economy.swift), which also reconciles all three surfaces a listing shows on (shelf, catalog, open detail) from the server's response.

**Forward-compatible with the un-rolled backend:** `status` and `viewCount` are optional-decoded (same convention as the presign `cacheControl` field), so the app runs against the currently-deployed jar — which is exactly how it was verified below.

**Verified (2026-07-25)** — backend `ModularityTests` green (Corretto 21, 1/1); iOS `xcodebuild -sdk iphonesimulator` → BUILD SUCCEEDED. **Simulator pass on the live (pre-V10) backend**, which doubles as the forward-compat test: signed in → Economy showed the `Selling` chip → the empty shelf rendered (0/0/0/0 + "Nothing listed yet") → published *Leica M6 rangefinder / $1,200* against prod → the chip became **`Selling 1`** and the shelf showed the row (LIVE pill, "Listed just now", 0 saves · 0 views) with the stat tiles at Live 1 → the edit form opened pre-filled, price round-tripped from cents as `1200` → "Mark sold" against the missing route **reverted the row to LIVE, left the counts untouched, and surfaced the failure banner** — the optimistic-rollback path, confirmed by a real failure rather than a mock.

**Two bugs this pass caught and fixed:** the notice banner rendered only in `EconomyView`, i.e. *behind* the seller sheet, so a refused edit failed silently — the sheet now renders its own; and it overlapped the header and stat tiles, so it's in the layout rather than over it. Also added `listingMessage(from:fallback:)`, which suppresses a 404 body — a route miss or a deleted listing otherwise showed the seller raw Spring text ("No static resource v1/economy/listings/…").

**Not covered / deferred:** photo *reordering* (you can add and remove; the first is the cover); no "price dropped" notification to people who saved the listing (`SOLD` would be a natural `notifications` consumer, alongside the existing `ListingCreated`); and the shelf is a single 100-item fetch with client-side filtering — fine now, but a seller past that needs the paged, server-filtered `/mine`.

**To deploy:** V10 is non-destructive (adds columns, backfills, leaves `active`), so it's the standard roll — push the image, then
```bash
aws ecs update-service --cluster gojogo --service gojogo-backend --force-new-deployment --region us-east-1
```
The mutation endpoints (`PUT …/{id}`, `PUT …/{id}/status`, `GET …/mine/stats`) are **unverified against a real backend** — they 404 until this lands. A two-user curl E2E in the style of the earlier 2b milestones should follow the deploy: owner edits, non-owner gets 403, status round-trip ACTIVE→SOLD→ACTIVE, sold listing absent from browse but present in `/mine` and `/saved`, view count bumping for a non-owner and not for the owner.

## Phase 2d — Watch backend + post deletion (deployed 2026-07-25)

Watch was the last vertical still running on the session cache. A video existed on the device that uploaded it and nowhere else; its "views" were that device's playbacks; its subscriber count was a hash of the channel name; and there was no way to delete one. This slice gives it a real service.

**Deployed:** ECS image `72d2554` (task def rev 7); Flyway `Migrating … to version "18 - watch"` → `now at version v18`. Unauthenticated `GET /v1/videos` → 401 (route exists; auth required).

**Why a new module and not `social`.** ARCHITECTURE.md §6a already listed `watch` as its own vertical. A video is not a post — it has a title, a description, a thumbnail, a duration and a view count that a post has none of, and folding it into `social.post` would have meant either nullable columns nobody else uses or a polymorphic comment table. It gets the `watch` schema and owns its own engagement.

**Subscribers are followers — one graph.** The tempting shortcut is a `watch.subscription` table. That would have produced two numbers that drift: follow someone on their profile and their videos would still say "0 subscribers". Instead `social` now exposes a read-only **`SocialGraphApi`** (`followeeIds`, batched `followerCounts`) and `watch` decorates every channel through it. Writes stay on social's REST surface, where `UserFollowed` is published and the notification is sent — a module that wants to *change* the graph sends the user there. On the client, Subscribe simply calls follow.

**Views are viewers, not playbacks.** `watch.video_view` is keyed `(video_id, viewer_id)`, so `POST /v1/videos/{id}/view` is idempotent: the first open per person counts, every one after is a no-op. That is both the figure a video product actually shows and the one a retrying client can't corrupt. The same `(video, user)` keying makes like and save idempotent. Note the assigned-id gotcha recorded below: `recordView` checks `existsById` *before* saving, because an `@IdClass` entity merges rather than inserts and a duplicate-key catch would never fire.

**Schema — `V18__watch.sql`.** `video` (kind LONG_FORM/SHORT, title, description, thumb_url, video_url, duration_seconds, three counts), `video_like`, `video_save`, `video_view`, `video_comment`, `video_comment_like`. Cross-schema references are plain UUIDs, as everywhere else. Playback is still the direct-S3 object a post's video already uses — the MediaConvert → HLS pipeline is Phase 3 and slots in behind `video_url` without touching this surface.

**iOS.** New `WatchStore` (feeds, publish, engagement, comments, DTO→model mapping) and `AppState+Watch.swift`. `refreshWatch()` is a real fetch of both feeds with keyset paging; publishing uploads the movie and the chosen cover then creates the row and swaps the optimistic local item for the server's, keeping the local poster bytes so the card doesn't blink. Every mutation is optimistic and each sync method no-ops for an id the server has never seen, so a clip whose upload is in flight — or one restored from a cache written before this — keeps working. Comments route by content type: a video id goes to the watch endpoints, a post id to social's.

**Post deletion.** `DELETE /v1/posts/{id}` has existed since M2 but nothing in the app called it — the only destructive action on a post was "Hide", which just removed it locally. Your own post now offers **Delete post** in the feed menu and the profile grid (someone else's still offers Hide / Report), behind a confirmation, with an optimistic removal that **re-inserts the post at its original index if the server refuses** rather than leaving you believing it's gone.

**Still to do:** a two-user curl E2E — publish, feed, distinct-viewer count across two accounts, subscriber count reflecting a follow, owner-only 403 on edit/delete, and the post-delete rollback. Clips published before this roll need the iOS flush-on-refresh (or a re-publish) to leave the device.

## Phase 2b · Milestone 6 — partner onboarding + KYC (deployed; live-verified 2026-07-30)

Delivery has been unusable since its demo catalog was deleted: the app could order food, but the only way a restaurant could exist was a Flyway migration. This milestone is merchant self-onboarding — the thing PROGRESS has called "the blocker for delivery being usable at all". No new AWS infra beyond one generated secret; `V19__partner.sql` runs on startup.

**Backend — `com.gojogo.partner` module** (new). It owns the *business relationship*, not anything the business sells: an application (business identity + contact + address), a set of KYC documents, and a status a human moves. Deliberately generic — `kind` is `RESTAURANT | DRIVER | COURIER`, because a driver's application is the same object with somewhere else to land.

Decisions worth keeping straight:

- **Nothing auto-approves.** There is no admin role in this app (the same constraint that left the music catalog without an ingest endpoint), so review sits on a token-guarded surface outside the JWT chain — `/v1/partner/admin/**`, guarded by `PARTNER_ADMIN_TOKEN`, 404 to anyone without it. Same shape as the economy cleanup endpoints, and the same reasoning. KYC a machine waves through is not KYC.
- **KYC documents are private, and that needed new plumbing.** Everything `/v1/media/presign` mints goes under `media/*`, which the bucket policy makes **world-readable** (the interim measure until CloudFront). That is right for a post photo and catastrophic for an ID card. New public [`MediaDocumentApi`](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/media/MediaDocumentApi.java) presigns into `private/partner/{userId}/…` instead — no `Cache-Control`, no public URL, not swept by the orphan job (which only knows `media/`), and readable only through a **10-minute presigned GET** minted per view for the reviewer. The row stores an **object key**, never a URL, and an attach whose key isn't under the applicant's own prefix is a 400. Document types are narrower than the public whitelist (jpeg/png/heic **+ pdf**, no video, no audio); the public presign still refuses a PDF.
- **`partner → delivery`, one way, public API only.** Approval calls [`MerchantProvisioningApi.provisionMerchant`](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/delivery/MerchantProvisioningApi.java) and gets a merchant id back. Delivery learns nothing about onboarding — the only trace is `owner_id` on the merchant row, which is the whole basis for "you may edit this restaurant". `ModularityTests` green.
- **Provisioning is idempotent**, so a retried approval (or a reviewer who clicks twice) can't leave a partner with two restaurants; approving an already-approved application is a 409 either way.
- **A provisioned restaurant starts closed.** A catalog entry with no dishes is a dead end for a buyer, so opening is the owner's act and delivery refuses it (409) until at least one dish is available. The reverse holds too: marking the last available dish sold out **closes the restaurant by itself** and says so, rather than leaving a live listing that 400s every order.
- **`suspended` is a second flag, not a reuse of `active`.** They answer different questions: `active` is the owner saying they're open, `suspended` is the platform saying they're not. Browse requires both. Restoring a suspended partner must not fling open a restaurant its owner had closed, and suspending one must not look, to them, like they closed it themselves.
- **A rejection is repaired, not replaced.** There is one application per person per kind; `REJECTED` goes back to editable with its documents and the reviewer's note intact, and resubmitting clears the old verdict. The note is written for the applicant to read — it's what the rejection push carries as its body.
- **`DRIVER`/`COURIER` are accepted and reviewed but can't be approved yet** (409 naming Phase 3 `dispatch`). Better a plain refusal than an approval that provisions nothing and leaves someone "approved" with no way to work. The app's existing driver/courier prototype (`partnerRoles`, `PartnerFlowView`) is untouched and still local-only.

Schema (`partner` schema; `user_id` and `provisioned_ref_id` are plain UUIDs — no FK crosses a schema):

| Table | Columns |
|---|---|
| `partner.partner_account` | id, user_id, kind, status, business_name, category, description, logo_url, contact_*, country/city/address_line, lat/lon, review_note, provisioned_ref_id, submitted_at, reviewed_at, created_at, updated_at — `UNIQUE (user_id, kind)`, CHECKs on kind/status and on "approved ⇔ provisioned" |
| `partner.partner_document` | id, account_id (FK, cascade), kind, **object_key**, content_type, uploaded_at — `UNIQUE (account_id, kind)`, so re-uploading a blurry ID replaces it |
| `delivery.merchant` | gains `owner_id` (nullable — the deleted V8 seed had none) and `suspended` |

**API surface** — applicant (Bearer-authed, all scoped to the caller):
- `GET /v1/partner/me?kind=RESTAURANT` → `{account: null}` until they've applied (the app's gate). Carries `requiredDocuments` / `missingDocuments` / `canEdit` / `canSubmit`, so the client renders the checklist rather than reimplementing the rule.
- `POST /v1/partner/applications` — **upsert**; there's at most one per person per kind, so the client never has to know whether it exists. 409 while under review or once approved.
- `POST …/{id}/documents/presign` → a private PUT · `PUT …/{id}/documents` (attach by key; replacing deletes the object it replaced) · `DELETE …/{id}/documents/{docId}`
- `POST …/{id}/submit` (400 naming exactly what's missing) · `POST …/{id}/withdraw` (back to editable, documents kept)

Reviewer (`X-Partner-Admin-Token`, outside the JWT chain):
- `GET /v1/partner/admin/applications?status=` (defaults to SUBMITTED) · `GET …/{id}` — both with a freshly signed link per document
- `POST …/{id}/approve` (also **restores** a suspended partner) · `POST …/{id}/reject` `{note}` · `POST …/{id}/suspend` `{note}`

Owner's restaurant (`delivery`, Bearer-authed, scoped to `/mine` so there is no id to authorise):
- `GET|PUT /v1/delivery/merchants/mine` · `PUT …/mine/open` `{open}` (409 on an empty menu or a suspension)
- `POST …/mine/sections` · `PUT|DELETE …/mine/sections/{id}` · `POST …/mine/sections/{id}/items` · `PUT|DELETE …/mine/items/{id}`
- Every mutation answers with **the whole restaurant**, so the editor re-renders from one authoritative response instead of reconciling a partial write.

**Not here on purpose: incoming orders.** A merchant can't accept or advance an order, because `OrderFulfilmentJob` walks every order along a simulated timeline — a kitchen button the job immediately overrules is worse than no button. That surface arrives with Phase 3 `dispatch`, which replaces the job. *(**It arrived in Phase 4 M1**, 2026-08-02, and turned out to be a precondition rather than a sequel: deleting the job removes the only thing that ever moved an order, so the kitchen had to become a person before the courier could.)*

**Events** — `partner.PartnerReviewed` (one event, not four: every consumer so far wants who / what happened / what to say). First consumer: `notifications`, via [PartnerNotificationListener](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/notifications/internal/PartnerNotificationListener.java) → a new `ApnsPushSender.notifySystem`. **Push only, no activity row** — the feed is built around an actor whose profile decorates the row, and here the platform decided. Unlike the chat listener it waits for the commit: partner writes are JPA, so an approval that rolls back must not have already told someone they're live.

**Fixed along the way — a pre-existing bug on every token-guarded path.** A `ResponseStatusException` is rendered by forwarding to `/error`, and Spring Security authorizes that ERROR dispatch too — so any 404/409 raised on a `permitAll` path came back as a **bodyless 401** from the bearer entry point. The economy cleanup endpoints had this since they shipped (PROGRESS documented a 404 they never actually returned). `SecurityConfig` now permits `/error`, which carries no data of its own. Verified: unknown application → `404 {"message":"No such application"}`, wrong token → `404 {"message":"No such endpoint"}`.

**iOS** — `PartnerModels` + `PartnerStore` (one store for both server modules, because it's one journey) + [AppState+Partner.swift](GojoGo/Models/AppState+Partner.swift).

> **Restaurant creation was removed from the app on 2026-07-27 — it happens in Gojo Admin.** Deleted from iOS: the application form, the KYC document checklist, the "in review" screen, and every store/AppState method behind them (`save`, `submit`, `withdraw`, document presign/attach/delete). What remains is the **merchant dashboard**: a `Your restaurant` chip that appears *only* for someone whose account is already provisioned, opening straight onto the storefront + menu editor. `AppState.merchantDashboardEnabled` turns even that off in one line if the dashboard moves to admin too — that call is deliberately deferred.
>
> **The backend is unchanged.** `partner` still owns the application, the private KYC upload, the review and the provisioning; iOS just stops calling the applicant half. **What Gojo Admin will need and does not have yet:** an admin-side create — the current applicant endpoints are caller-scoped (`POST /v1/partner/applications` writes *your own* application), so an operator can't fill one in on a merchant's behalf. The natural shape is `POST /v1/partner/admin/applications` taking the owner's profile id, plus an admin document-upload path, both on the existing token-guarded surface. Small slice, not yet built.

Entry is a chip in the GojoDelivery header that reads the journey back to you (*Add your restaurant → Finish signup → In review → Action needed → Your restaurant*, with a live dot when open), beside the existing courier chip; the empty catalog now points at it. [MerchantPartnerView](GojoGo/Screens/MerchantPartnerView.swift) is one sheet with three faces — the application form with a document checklist (each row a `PhotosPicker` that re-encodes to JPEG, since the private whitelist is narrower), an "in review" screen with a withdraw button, and the dashboard. [MerchantDashboardView](GojoGo/Screens/MerchantDashboardView.swift) is the storefront card + open/closed switch + stat tiles + the menu editor (sections, dishes, sold-out toggle, delete-with-confirmation), plus a storefront form and a dish form. Nothing is optimistic except the open switch — a menu editor showing a dish that didn't save is how a customer orders something that doesn't exist — and that switch reverts, loudly, when the server refuses.

**Also added:** a DEBUG-only `GG_API_BASE_URL` override in [BackendConfig](GojoGo/CoreNetworking/BackendConfig.swift), so a simulator build can be pointed at a backend running on the Mac and a milestone's UI can be driven **before** it's deployed (which is how the pass below was done). Release builds ignore it entirely.

```bash
SIMCTL_CHILD_GG_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 xcrun simctl launch <udid> com.gojo.gojogo
```
(The `SIMCTL_CHILD_*` vars go in simctl's **own** environment — passed as trailing arguments they become argv and are silently ignored, which cost a debugging round here and is worth knowing for the `GG_AUTOLOGIN_*` hook too.)

**Verified (2026-07-27)** — `ModularityTests` green (Corretto 21); iOS `xcodebuild -sdk iphonesimulator` → BUILD SUCCEEDED; `cdk synth GojoGoFargateStack` clean. **Two-user E2E, 83/83 green** (`verify_partner.sh`, session-local) against the real Spring app on a local Postgres 18 migrated **V1→V19 from scratch**, with Cognito tokens for the existing test users and presign hitting the real S3 bucket: A applies → submitting without papers is refused, naming them → each document presigns into `private/partner/{userId}/`, uploads (200), is **403 on a public GET** and attaches → attaching a key under someone else's prefix is a 400 → the public presign still refuses a PDF (415) → A submits, the form locks, editing under review is a 409, B touching A's application is a 404 → the wrong admin token is a 404, the right one lists A with a signed link that **actually reads the paper** (200) → reject with a note → A sees REJECTED, the reason, an editable form and both documents still there → resubmit clears the verdict → approve provisions the merchant → approving twice is a 409 → A manages a restaurant (closed, empty menu), B manages nothing (404) and can't edit A's (404) → opening an empty menu is a 409 → A edits the storefront, adds a section and two dishes and opens → B finds it in browse with the sold-out dish hidden → **B orders two tagines and the server prices it** (2×1450 + 0 fee + 99 service = 2999), a sold-out dish is a 409 → marking the last available dish sold out **closes the restaurant and drops it out of browse** → deleting the section takes its dishes and **B's receipt keeps the name and the price they paid** → suspend hides it from browse while A still sees it flagged with their own switch untouched and the menu intact, and A can't reopen it (409) → approve restores it. The same suite ran 82/83 on the **v16→v19 upgrade path** (the shape prod is in), the one failure being a test expectation about tag ordering — see the limits below.

**Simulator pass on that backend** (via the new `GG_API_BASE_URL` override): the header chip read **Add your restaurant** and the empty catalog pointed at it → the application form saved to the local backend → for the approved account the chip became **Your restaurant** with a live dot and **Dar Zellij** appeared in the buyer-facing catalog (*Moroccan · Tagine · Delivery free*, 4.5, 32 min) → the dashboard showed the storefront card, the "Taking orders" switch and the stat tiles → a dish added through the editor with the price typed **"14,50"** round-tripped as `1450` cents and the tiles moved 1→2 → marking both dishes sold out **closed the restaurant with its notice** ("nothing on the menu is available") and the optimistic re-open **reverted** against the 409 → a suspension applied behind the app's back rendered the Suspended card with **the reviewer's own words**.

**Two bugs this pass caught and fixed:** the DTOs declared `Date` fields, but the shared decoder has no date strategy and the backend sends nanosecond fractions — decoding failed *silently* in the store, so the app looked signed-out-of-partner while the endpoint was answering fine (the codebase convention is a raw `String` parsed with `BackendDate`, now followed). And the dashboard's pull-to-refresh re-read only the restaurant, so a suspension showed the fallback text instead of the reviewer's note — it now refreshes the account too, and reacts when the flag flips underneath it.

**Known limits.** Merchant **tags and browse categories don't preserve the order the owner typed** — they're a JPA `Set`, so they come back in an arbitrary order (pre-existing for the seeded rows; it only becomes visible now that owners author them). Fixing it means a `sort_order` column and an `@OrderColumn`. Also: no opening *hours* (just an open/closed switch), no dish options or extras, no photo reordering, no per-merchant order list (see above), and the merchant's lat/lon comes from the application rather than a map pin.

**To deploy:** V19 is additive (new schema, two nullable/defaulted columns on `delivery.merchant`), so it's the standard roll — but it needs **one CDK deploy first**, because the review token is a new Secrets Manager secret:

```bash
cd infra && cdk deploy GojoGoFargateStack \
  -c imageTag=$(../scripts/deploy-backend.sh --current) \
  -c domainName=api.gojogo.app -c certificateArn=<arn>
cd .. && ./scripts/deploy-backend.sh
aws secretsmanager get-secret-value --secret-id gojogo/partner-admin-token \
  --query SecretString --output text      # this is your X-Partner-Admin-Token
```

Unlike `ECONOMY_ADMIN_TOKEN` — which is passed per-deploy precisely so a plain deploy turns cleanup back *off* — this one is a **generated secret that persists**: approving merchants is ongoing work, and a deploy that quietly emptied it would take the review queue offline and, with it, any way for delivery to gain a restaurant. Keep it out of the repo. After the roll, the E2E above should be re-run against prod, and the first real merchant walked through the queue by hand.

## Phase 2c — Full Instagram stories (deployed; live-verified 2026-07-30)

Stories existed since Phase 1 M2 but were a stub: one image URL per frame, a 24h expiry, and a seen flag. This slice builds the actual product. It stays entirely inside the `social` module — no new AWS infra, no new module, and it reuses `media` presign for every upload.

**The one product decision worth recording.** A story reply is *not* routed into a My World DM. My World is a separate, phone-verified identity with its own profile (and will get its own stories), so a reply to a *social* story landing in a My World thread would cross two identities that the app deliberately keeps apart. Replies are therefore **story comments owned by `social`**, read back with Instagram's privacy: the frame's author sees every reply, any other viewer sees only the ones they sent. It's a real row in `social.story_comment`, so making replies public later is a change to one query, not a migration.

**Schema — four migrations.**
- `V11__story_media.sql` — `story_frame` gains `media_type` (IMAGE/VIDEO/TEXT), `video_url`, `duration_ms`, `caption`, `overlays_json`, `background`, `audience`, `deleted_at`; `image_url` drops NOT NULL (a text card has no media) and a CHECK enforces "the right media for the type" instead. `story_view` gains `viewed_at` (the viewers list is ordered by it). **Deletion is soft** — a highlight keeps a frame alive past its expiry and `story_view` cascades, so a hard delete would silently take the seen-state and the viewers list with it.
- `V12__story_engagement.sql` — `story_reaction` (PK frame+viewer, so a second emoji replaces the first), `story_comment`, `story_mute` (its own table, not a flag on `follow`: you can mute someone you don't follow, and an unfollow/refollow shouldn't silently un-mute them).
- `V13__story_notifications.sql` — `notification.story_frame_id`, because a story notification points at a frame, not a post.
- `V14__story_highlights.sql` — `close_friend` (one-directional, indexed both ways since rings asks "whose list am I on?"), `story_highlight`, `story_highlight_frame`.

**API** (all under the existing auth): `GET /v1/stories` (rings — now ordered you → unseen → seen → muted, with viewer/reply counts on your own frames only) · `POST /v1/stories` (frame specs; **still accepts the old `frameImageUrls` body** so an app build from before this roll keeps posting) · `POST /v1/stories/frames/{id}/seen` · `DELETE /v1/stories/frames/{id}` · `PUT|DELETE /v1/stories/frames/{id}/reaction` · `GET|POST /v1/stories/frames/{id}/replies` · `DELETE /v1/stories/replies/{id}` · `GET /v1/stories/frames/{id}/viewers` (owner-only) · `POST|DELETE /v1/stories/mute/{profileId}` · `GET /v1/stories/archive` · `GET|POST|PUT|DELETE /v1/stories/highlights[/{id}]` · `GET|PUT /v1/stories/close-friends`.

**Module boundary.** `social` publishes `StoryReacted` / `StoryReplied`; `notifications` consumes both (its third and fourth event contract) into activity rows plus an APNs alert. `ModularityTests` green — no new cross-module reach.

**Overlays are a spec, never burned in.** Text and stickers are stored as normalized JSON (position 0…1 of the canvas, font size as a fraction of its width) and rendered live at view time. Flattening them into the image was the alternative, but a video frame can't be flattened without re-encoding it — one render path for all three kinds beats two. The backend treats `overlays_json` as opaque, the same way `messaging.ConversationContext` is opaque to messaging.

**iOS** — new `StoriesStore` (split out of `SocialStore`, which was about to double), `AppState+Stories.swift`, `StoryModels.swift`, and screens: `StoryComposer` (gallery / camera / text card, draggable-pinchable-rotatable overlays, audience toggle), `StoryCanvas` (the shared renderer — the composer previews the *actual* thing the viewer plays), a rewritten `StoryViewer` (video, overlays, reply bar, reaction row, "Seen by", owner menu), `StoryInsightsSheet`, `StoryArchiveView` + highlight editor/picker, `CloseFriendsView`. Highlights render on `ProfileView`; the Home rail and browser gained a close-friends badge and mute on long-press.

**Two things worth knowing about the design.** Text-card backgrounds are **tonal, not colorful** — the app's palette is monochrome by design (`GGColor`), so Instagram's rainbow gradients would have been the only saturated surface in the product; same reason the close-friends marker is a glyph rather than a green ring. Say the word if you want real color here and it's a one-file change (`StoryBackground`).

**Verified (2026-07-25)** — backend compiles + `ModularityTests` green on Corretto 21; iOS `xcodebuild -sdk iphonesimulator` → BUILD SUCCEEDED; simulator pass on the **live pre-V11 backend**: the composer opens from Home and the browser, the text card renders and auto-sizes live, the audience toggle flips, and Archive / Close friends open with their empty states. Posting correctly **failed and rolled back with a notice** — the old backend rejects the new body, which is exactly the intended path and also proves the optimistic-insert revert. Fixed during that pass: source-button labels wrapped at 402pt, and the archive/close-friends/composer sheets couldn't present while the stories browser sheet was up (they now declare their own copies, the same way `ProfileView` already does for the post viewer).

### Music on a story

Sound is a **platform** capability, not a story feature — hence a new `com.gojogo.music` module (`music` schema, `V15`) rather than a column on `story_frame`. Shorts and posts are the obvious next consumers; stories are just the first.

- `GET /v1/music/tracks?q=&limit=` — trending (hand `trending_rank` first, then `use_count`) when `q` is blank, otherwise a title/artist match · `GET /v1/music/tracks/{id}`.
- Public `MusicApi` (`find` / `recordUse`) is the only way `social` touches the catalog — the same seam `economy → MessagingApi` uses. `ModularityTests` confirms it.
- `V16` adds a **snapshot** of the track to `story_frame` (`music_title`/`artist`/`artwork_url`/`audio_url` + the clip window), not a foreign key: no FK may cross a schema (ARCHITECTURE §4), a story must keep playing the sound it was posted with after a track is pulled, and drawing the music sticker must not need a cross-module read. A CHECK stops a half-populated snapshot.
- **The client sends only `musicTrackId` + the window.** Title, artist, artwork and audio URL are all resolved server-side, so a frame can't claim to be a song it isn't. The clip is clamped to ≤15s and to the track's real length.
- iOS: `MusicStore`, `MusicPickerSheet` (search + preview, then a waveform scrubber for the 15s window), `StoryMusicSticker`, and `StoryMusicController` (seeks to the clip start, loops on a boundary observer). A frame with a sound plays its **video muted** — two audio tracks at once is noise. A still carrying a sound holds the screen for the clip's length instead of 5s. The waveform is deterministic from the track id: real sample analysis would mean downloading and decoding the whole file just to draw a scrubber.

**Seeding the music catalog — your call, and a licensing one.** The table ships **empty** and there is deliberately **no ingest endpoint**: this app has no admin role, so a catalog writer would be unauthenticated. Upload an audio file through the existing media presign (or straight to S3), then insert the row:

```sql
INSERT INTO music.track (title, artist, artwork_url, audio_url, duration_ms, trending_rank)
VALUES ('Track title', 'Artist', 'https://…/artwork.jpg', 'https://…/audio.m4a', 214000, 1);
```

`is_active = false` pulls a track from search **without** breaking the stories that already carry its snapshot. Only add music you hold the rights to distribute — nothing in this code checks that, and the app is the distributor.

**Placeholder tracks are currently seeded (`V17`).** Four tracks by "GojoGo Sound" — `Night Drive`, `Casablanca Blue`, `Atlas Morning`, `Medina Late` — live in the catalog so the picker can be tested. They are **synthesised**: generated from sine/saw math with no source recording, sample pack or third-party audio anywhere in them, so there is nothing to license. Audio and cover art sit in the media bucket under `media/music/`. They sound like placeholder loops because that is what they are — replace them before anyone real sees the app:

```sql
UPDATE music.track SET is_active = false WHERE artist = 'GojoGo Sound';  -- hide
DELETE FROM music.track WHERE artist = 'GojoGo Sound';                   -- or drop
```

Dropping them is safe: no foreign key points at a track, and any story that used one keeps its own snapshot and goes on playing.

**Verified against a real backend (2026-07-25) — two-user E2E, 58/58 green.** Run on a **local Postgres 18** with all sixteen migrations applied from scratch (`V1`→`V16`, "Successfully applied 16 migrations… now at version v16") and the real Spring app booted against it, so Hibernate `ddl-auto: validate` accepted every new mapping — the `@Embedded` music snapshot, both enums, and all the V11–V14 columns. Auth used real Cognito tokens for the existing test users. Script: scratchpad `e2e_stories.sh`.

**The upgrade path was tested separately, which matters more than the fresh install** — prod already holds story rows, and `V11` alters that table. A second database was taken to `V10` (the shape prod is in today), seeded with legacy image-only frames and a `story_view` with no timestamp, and then `V11`→`V16` were applied over it: legacy frames come out `media_type=IMAGE` / `audience=ALL` / no music, the legacy view gets a backfilled `viewed_at`, all four new CHECK constraints apply cleanly against the existing rows, and a legacy row is still updatable afterwards (no CHECK trap on soft delete).

What it covers: the music catalog (trending order, search by artist, a pulled `is_active=false` track hidden from browse *and* rejected on attach) · frame creation for IMAGE/TEXT with per-kind 400s · **the legacy `frameImageUrls` body still mapping to an IMAGE frame**, which is what makes the rolling deploy safe · music snapshots resolved server-side (title/artist/audio URL come from the catalog, never the client) with the clip clamped to 15s *and* to a 9s track's real length · `viewerCount` present for the author and null for everyone else · seen-state not duplicating on a re-mark (the `existsById` guard) · reactions replacing rather than stacking, visible as `myReaction` only to the reactor · **reply privacy — the author reads both replies, B reads only their own** · close friends gating a restricted frame and *revoking* access when B is removed · mute flagging the ring, sorting it last, and leaving it reachable · a deleted frame leaving the ring while its highlight keeps playing (what the soft delete is for) · 403s for reading another author's viewers, deleting their frame, or highlighting it · the archive excluding deleted frames and being own-only.

**Deployed to prod and re-verified against it (2026-07-25).** The push to `main` auto-deployed via the Actions workflow — so the CI path works and the `iam:PassRole` grant is confirmed good. All sixteen migrations applied to prod on first boot. With the placeholder catalog seeded (below), the iOS surfaces that had never been exercised with real data were walked in the simulator **against prod**: the picker lists tracks with artwork streaming from S3, the clip scrubber drags and reports its window, the music sticker renders on the frame, and posting a story with a sound round-trips — a clip scrubbed to ~0:15 came back from `GET /v1/stories` as `startMs=15985, durationMs=15000`, with title/artist resolved server-side.

**Still unverified:** video story frames. The `mp4/mov` presign path is the one posts already use, but the story-specific poster → stream swap and the progress-driven bar have only run locally — a real video story has never been posted.

## Phase 3 · Milestone 2 — Driver onboarding completes (deployed 2026-08-01)

**What was missing.** M1 gave an approved driver somewhere to land. What sat between "I'd like to drive" and an approval worth granting was a **convincing mime**: the app's stake page slept for 1.6 seconds and charged nothing, "submit" inserted a role into a local `Set`, and the vehicle fields — make, model, plate, and two tiles you tapped to *claim* you'd photographed the papers — went nowhere at all. Exactly the pattern the identity half lost when Sumsub arrived, and for the same reason: **a checkbox that says a photo was taken is worse than no photo, because it looks like evidence.**

**The stake is a ledger movement, and it stays the applicant's money.** `payments` has buckets precisely for this: the $30 moves from the driver's own AVAILABLE into their own **STAKING**, out of circulation without changing hands ([PartnerStakeService](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/partner/internal/PartnerStakeService.java)). That is what makes returning it a *release* rather than a refund, and what will let Phase 3 M5 pay a passenger's verification reward out of it as a transfer rather than an invention. Five columns on `partner_account` record that a movement happened; **they are not a second copy of the balance**, which would eventually disagree with the ledger.

**It gates submission, not approval** (SPECS §4) — staking is what funds verification, so it happens before a human spends time on the application rather than after, and the applicant finds out while they can still act. Same reasoning that put the ID check at submission in 2b M6. The **KYC fee comes out of the stake**, not out of the wallet: billing a separate balance would leave somebody able to stake and then have nothing to check them with. It is capped at what is left, so a config mistake can make the fee useless but never puts a stake into debt.

**Every movement's idempotency key is derived from a fact already on the row** — `partner:{id}:stake`, and for the fee the *total charged so far*, so a retried submission collapses onto one movement while a genuine resubmission (which moved the total) gets its own. Nothing is keyed off a clock or a random. And a **zero fee writes no entry at all**: a ledger line whose only content is that a policy is switched off is noise in a statement somebody has to read.

**Who gets their stake back, and when.** A rejection and a withdrawal both release it — keeping it would make refusing people profitable, which is not a pressure a review queue should be under. A **suspension deliberately does not**: the stake is what a penalty and a verification reward are paid from, and handing it back at the moment somebody is in trouble defeats the mechanism.

**Vehicles live in `partner`, not in `dispatch`** (`partner.vehicle`, `V30`). A vehicle is a *claim a human reviews* — papers, expiry dates, a plate to match against a photo — which is what this module is for. Dispatch needs one fact out of all of it, the category, and gets it through the provisioning API at approval; putting the table there would have made the matching module responsible for insurance certificates. Three rules carry the weight, each answering a way this gets gamed or simply goes stale:

- **Editing the plate or the category un-approves the vehicle.** A driver who changes the registration on a car a reviewer already looked at has described a different car, and keeping the old verdict is exactly the shape of the fraud this check exists to catch. Repainting it is not, so a colour edit keeps the approval.
- **Exactly one vehicle is active**, held by a *partial unique index* rather than by the service — "activate this one" is two writes, and only the database can hold the invariant between them. The plate is unique **per region**, never globally: the same string is a different car in another country, and a global index would refuse the second market this platform opens in.
- **Papers expire, and the vehicle knows when.** Dates, not timestamps — a policy lapses on a day in a timezone nobody recorded, and pretending to a second's precision would be a lie a sweep then acts on. [VehicleExpiryJob](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/partner/internal/VehicleExpiryJob.java) is the only thing in the system that would ever notice: without it a registration that ran out in March goes on receiving work forever, because *the day nothing happened* is not an event anything reacts to. A 7-day grace (**CONFIG**) is deliberate — renewals are slow and cutting somebody's income at the exact midnight a certificate expires is not a policy anybody could defend — and flagging the **active** vehicle suspends its driver in the same pass, because a flag that leaves work arriving is a flag that did nothing.

**A driver needs a vehicle and a courier does not**, and the asymmetry is the point: a courier on a bicycle has no registration and no insurance certificate to show, and demanding one would exclude the most common way that job is actually done. A courier who *does* register a scooter still gets it reviewed — optional, not ignored. It is a config flag, so a market that disagrees changes a row.

**Provisioning now names the active vehicle's category**, replacing M1's null-and-default: what dispatch matches on is the car they said they are driving, not the one they own most of. A flagged active vehicle names *no* category rather than one dispatch must not send work for.

**iOS — the flow stops lying, and the meaning of "submit" changes.** Every step is now a server call: the application is a real DRAFT (`POST /v1/partner/applications`, kind `DRIVER`), the stake page reads the **server's amount, the wallet's real balance and the shortfall** — which is what turns an empty wallet into a top-up button for exactly the missing amount instead of a dead end — and the vehicle form gained region and both expiry dates, with the two capture tiles replaced by **real uploads into the private prefix** (the vehicle is saved first if it does not exist yet, because a document belongs to a car). The done page no longer says "you're now a driver": **submitting queues you**, in the same human queue a restaurant has waited in since 2b M6, and the screen says a person checks every application. `partnerRoles` is read from `/v1/me/roles` — which reads the dispatch registry — instead of being a local `Set.insert`, so the app and the platform cannot disagree about whether somebody may work, and a suspension takes effect on the next launch rather than the next reinstall.

**Driver Mode goes live for a real driver, and stays a demo for everybody else.** The toggle calls `/v1/dispatch/me/availability`, positions go up while available at the interval **the server asks for** (`LiveLocationProvider` gained real tracking — the one-shot path hands back a fix up to a minute old, and dispatch treats a position that age as no position, so a driver would sit invisible in the middle of a city), offers arrive from `/v1/dispatch/me` into the card the dashboard already draws, and accept/decline are the real endpoints. Losing a race is reported in the server's words rather than hidden. **The demo is not dead code**: somebody never approved has no other way to see what the screen is for, and the branch is on *the registry* rather than a debug flag, so the one thing that cannot happen is a real driver being shown an invented offer.

Two things the live path deliberately does **not** do. The offer card shows a distance and a pickup and **no fare** — dispatch never sees money, and a plausible number there would be the worst kind of wrong, so `fareLabel` renders "—". And a real acceptance **ends at the acceptance**: what happens after belongs to the vertical that asked, and none publishes a job yet (rides are M3, real couriers Phase 4 M1), so the demo's animated route is not run over a real assignment. Inventing a trip is the one thing worse than an empty dashboard.

**Verified:** `mvn test` **183/183** on Corretto 21 — `ModularityTests` green, including the new `partner → payments` and `partner → config` edges (public API only) — plus **27 new tests**. 12 on the stake, all about money that must *not* move: paying twice moving once, an empty wallet recording nothing (an application that believes it is staked while the ledger disagrees is the one state that must not exist), a zero fee writing no entry, a fee bigger than the stake taking only what is left, a release paying out once, and the shortfall never going negative. 15 on vehicles, led by the one that matters — editing a plate un-approving, editing a colour not — plus flag/resubmit, the earlier of two expiry dates winning, missing dates being *incomplete* rather than *expired*, a courier needing no vehicle, and a flagged vehicle naming no category to dispatch. iOS **BUILD SUCCEEDED**.

**Deployed** 2026-08-01 with M1, as task definition **rev 27** / image `b3901f6`. No `--infra`: this milestone adds no secret, no env var and no IAM action. Flyway applied `V29` then `V30` at startup (prod was at v28) and health answered 200 through the ALB, which is a real database round-trip. ~~Still unexercised end to end in prod~~ — **exercised 2026-08-01** (see Phase 3 M4's prod E2E): a driver was approved, the $30 stake moved against a live wallet (AVAILABLE 5000 → 2000, 3000 locked in STAKING), the $5 ID-check fee came out of the *stake* and not the wallet, and a vehicle was reviewed and approved with its registration and insurance. All of it behaved as designed on the first run.

**What this does not do.** Community vehicle verification is Phase 3 M5 — the `COMMUNITY_VERIFIED` state exists now so the badge has somewhere to come from and a reviewer's vocabulary doesn't change under them later, but nothing sets it. Vehicle **photos** have a table, a cap and reference tracking but no iOS picker yet (the papers were the blocking half). Ride-hailing tokens are M4. Stake release on *account closure* (SPECS §4's "30 days, no open disputes") is not built: rejection and withdrawal are the live paths, and winding a driver down has trips and money in it.

## Phase 3 · Milestone 1 — The platform `dispatch` module (deployed 2026-08-01)

**What was missing.** Two things had been waiting on this module by name. A `DRIVER` or `COURIER` application could be filed, ID-verified and reviewed since 2b M6, and an approval then **refused with a 409** — there was nowhere for an approved driver to land. And GojoDelivery's courier is a name, a rating and a vehicle **invented by a scheduled job** walking a timeline, because no real person could be offered the work. This milestone is the landing place and the offer book: who is out there, where they are right now, and which job has been offered to whom.

**The module knows a kind, an id, a place and a category — and nothing else.** New platform `dispatch` module ([package-info](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/dispatch/package-info.java), `dispatch` schema, `V29__dispatch.sql`). There is no fare column and no order status, because a `DispatchRequest` that carried a price would make dispatch a party to the transaction and then two modules would own what a trip is worth. That narrowness is what lets one module serve both verticals: a rider looking for a car and a restaurant looking for a courier are the same search with different filters, and building it twice would guarantee two answers to "is this driver busy".

**One registry with a kind, not `driver` and `courier` side by side.** They differ in which requests reach them and in nothing else — same presence, same offer book, same counters — so two tables would mean two of every query above them. A dual-mode person (the vision's "switch between roles") is one human with **two rows**, which is also what makes *an accepted job in either mode makes you busy in both* a one-line rule instead of a cross-table invariant. The same reasoning makes a position report fan out to every registration the person holds: there is one of them and one phone.

**Presence is Postgres, and that is a deliberate deviation from SPECS §3**, which specifies Redis GEO — correct at a scale where thousands of workers are moving. There are zero today, an ElastiCache cluster bills by the hour whether or not anybody is driving, and the read this module performs is "available workers of kind K within N km", over a table already narrowed by kind and status. So the database runs a **bounding box** and Java measures the real distance over what comes back. Moving the presence columns to Redis later changes one repository method and no caller.

That box is the one piece of arithmetic whose bugs would be *invisible* — a window a fraction too small silently loses the nearest driver, and nothing anywhere logs "somebody was two hundred metres away and we didn't ask them". So it is padded by a tenth of a percent and tested all the way round the ring rather than at the four points a careless test would pick. **The padding is not superstition**: the box comes from `r/R` in radians and the true edge of the circle comes out of `asin`, and at bearing 180° on a 7 km ring the two disagreed in the last bit — a real test failure, caught before it was a lost driver.

**Ranking is a pure function, on purpose** ([`CandidateRanker`](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/dispatch/internal/CandidateRanker.java)). Nothing on this machine can execute SQL, and "who should get this trip" is the last logic in the system that should first run in production. Three terms, each normalised before it is weighted (a raw kilometre and a raw star are not comparable): **proximity 60, rating 25, idle time 15**. The third is the one that matters and the one a simpler system omits — without it the nearest driver takes every job on a quiet afternoon and the next one starves. The tests pin the trade-off in *both* directions: between two drivers about as close as each other an hour of waiting wins, and across a two-kilometre gap it does not, because fairness between drivers is a tiebreak and the passenger standing in the street outranks it.

**The offer book is where the damage would be**, so the states are kept apart deliberately:

- **`WITHDRAWN` is not `EXPIRED`.** Both end with the worker not doing the job, but only one is *about* the worker: a lapsed offer says they ignored their phone, a withdrawn one says somebody else was quicker. Counting the second against an acceptance rate would punish people for losing races nobody told them they were in.
- **Nobody is asked about the same job twice**, however many rings run. A second wave that re-includes the driver who just declined is the fastest way to teach them to close the app.
- **Rates are `null` until there is something to divide by.** "0% acceptance" on a driver's first day reads as an accusation, and a rate over no offers is not a rate.
- **A new worker's 5.00 is display, not a rating.** The first real rating replaces it rather than averaging with it — otherwise every driver starts with a free five stars. (My own test asserted the averaging version and was wrong; the code was right.)

**First acceptance wins, and the database is the arbiter.** Two couriers each believing an order is theirs is the single failure this module exists to prevent. The service checks the job state, and then a JPA `@Version` on the job row settles the case the check cannot see — two accepts a millisecond apart both pass an in-memory test and exactly one survives the version column. The loser gets a plain 409 rather than a quiet overwrite, every other open offer becomes `WITHDRAWN` in the same transaction, and no `DispatchAssigned` is published for a race that was lost.

**Waves are a poller, not a timer per request** ([`DispatchMatchingJob`](backend/src/main/java/com/gojogo/dispatch/internal/DispatchMatchingJob.java)) — the same shape as delivery's fulfilment job and for the same reason: every decision is recomputed from the row, so a missed run, a restart or a slow deploy catches up instead of leaving a rider watching a wave scheduled inside a process that has since died. Rings 1 → 3 → 7 km, 15s apart, wave size 5, offer TTL 30s, request TTL 5 min — **all of it in the config registry** (SPECS §14), so widening the search on a busy Friday is a row and not a deploy. Only the poll interval is in `application.yml`, because that one is infrastructure.

The same `next_wave_at` column is the whole of SPECS §3's *approaching readiness* trigger: a courier search that must not start until the kitchen is nearly done is simply a job whose first wave is in the future, and a scheduled ride is the same thing with a longer wait. Its TTL runs from the start of the search rather than from the booking, so an order dispatched twenty minutes early can't expire before anybody has been asked.

**Results come back as events, never as a call.** `DispatchAssigned` and `DispatchExhausted`; the vertical listens. A platform module that knew the name of `travel` or `delivery` would be a platform module edited every time one is added. `DispatchExhausted` carries **how many people saw it** — zero and fourteen are different failures ("there is nobody here" vs "nobody wants this trip"). `notifications` consumes `DispatchOffered` and pushes: an offer lives thirty seconds, so a driver with the phone in their pocket misses every one of them unless something rings. Push only, no activity row — by the time anybody scrolled back the work would be gone, and a permanent record of jobs you didn't get is a feed nobody wants.

**Two wirings out of the module:**

- **`partner` provisions into it.** `PartnerKind.isProvisionable()` is now true for all three kinds and `approve()` routes by kind through `DriverProvisioningApi` / `CourierProvisioningApi` — the same one-way `partner → vertical` shape `MerchantProvisioningApi` has had since 2b M6, and suspension/restore route the same way. The registration deliberately passes a **null category**: approving somebody says they may drive, not what they drive, and Phase 3 M2 sets it when a vehicle is registered. Until then dispatch fills in the default for the kind.
- **`/v1/me/roles` reads the registry.** `isDriver`/`isCourier` came from "has an approved application of that kind"; they now come from `DispatchApi.rolesOf`. The two agree today and will not the first time an approval provisions and something downstream of it fails — which is what SPECS §8 means by roles being *derived from the thing itself*. Suspension is not filtered out: a suspended driver is still a driver, and an app that stopped saying so would have nowhere to explain why no work is arriving.

**Positions arrive over REST, not the WebSocket** — the second deviation from SPECS §3. The socket that exists is API Gateway with `$connect`/`$disconnect` Lambdas owning a connection registry; there is **no inbound message route**, and building one belongs in the milestone where positions are *watched* (M3's live trip), not in the one where they are merely stored. `POST /v1/dispatch/me/position` returns **204 with no body** — it is the highest-frequency call in the system by an order of magnitude, and returning state nobody asked for would multiply that by whatever the state costs to build.
