The other candidate is still **disputes** (SPECS §5): the gap that means a settled order has no refund path, and the thing the proof photo has been collecting evidence *for* since M2. It is now also the gap that a *collection* has — a bag handed over at a counter settles the money the same way a doorstep does.

**What Phase 2f leaves on the table instead of a decision:** it is code-complete but **entirely unexercised** — nothing in the graph, fan-out, feed, circles or rich profile has run against the deployed backend even once — and it is **gated on SNS SMS** (#3) in a way no other milestone is. The code works against the `424242` dev bypass; in production, only verified test numbers can join a graph at all. Real phone verification is now the difference between a built product and a usable one, which is what moves #3 from "a launch blocker" to the single thing standing in front of a whole product area.

**Phase 4 M1 has its own unexercised paths, and they are worse than M5's in one specific way: they can strand food.** Nothing in it has run against prod, and unlike a ride, a delivery has a third party in it who is already cooking. Worth running by hand after the deploy, in this order: a **merchant accept** (does a courier search actually open, and does its `startAfter` land where the prep estimate says), an **exhausted search** (easy to produce — nobody is registered as a courier in prod today, so every order will take this path until somebody is), the **timeout sweep** (the money must come back, and this is the first sweep in the system that releases an escrow hold), and a full **accept → assign → collect → deliver**, which is the only way to see the courier's two ledger lines land on a person. A run needs an approved COURIER, which nothing in prod has ever had.

**Phase 4 M2's own unexercised paths, and one of them has a demonstrated ability to be wrong.** Nothing in it has run against the deployed backend, and no simulator pass was possible while the endpoints did not exist. In order: a **wrong pickup code** (the message lands under the field and the typed digits survive), **three empty PIN submissions** reaching the photo fallback — the path that looks like a bug until you have stood outside a flat where nobody is answering — and a **PHOTO order end to end**, which matters most because the first iOS build of that screen dead-ended it: the app offered a PIN field for a PIN nobody would ever read out. That was caught and fixed at integration by putting `handoffMode` on the courier's job, and a path that has been broken once is the path to drive first. On the money side, a **withdrawal** has never been attempted by anybody — and note it inherits "Needs YOU" #9: the Connect transfer itself is still unexercised, so the first worker payout is also the first payout of any kind.

**Small follow-ons the last three milestones left on the table**, none of them blocking: a **courier rating** (M1 deliberately does not feed the order rating to `dispatch.complete`, and M2 did not revisit it, so couriers still have no rating input at all), **QR scanning** for the pickup code (it already renders as one on the kitchen's screen; only the courier-side camera is missing), **disputes** (SPECS §5 — the thing a proof photo is evidence *for*, and the gap that means a settled order still has no refund path), passenger photos on a vehicle verification (the upload path exists; no migration needed), a share link for a *delivery* (`delivery` implements `ShareableResource` and nothing else changes), and universal-link routing for `https://gojogo.app/s/{token}` — the endpoint is live, the association file is an account decision.

<details>
<summary>The older resume notes, kept for the runbooks and the reasoning in them</summary>


**Phase 2 is complete and deployed** — M1 (My World messaging + setup), M2 (notifications), M3 (APNs push + reply/typing/send-later polish), M4 (chat attachments + socket resilience), M5 (chat-message push + persisted read receipts). APNs is activated (key in Secrets Manager, verified against Apple for both activity-feed *and* chat pushes); only a physical-device test remains (enable Push on the App ID, run on a device). **Phase 2b is under way** — M1 (economy marketplace), M2 (seller chat over the messaging API), M3 (listing context on the thread), and M4 (delivery vertical) are live.

**2e M1 and 2e M2 are deployed and prod-verified (24/24 each).** The plan's order from here is **2e M3** (a `platform-admin` Cognito group honored by `/v1/partner/admin/**`, plus the admin-side create the applicant endpoints can't do), then **2e M3** (payments + the GoJo Wallet, which every later pillar blocks on).

**Next 2b slices, in the order they make sense:**
1. **Deploy M6 (partner onboarding)** — built and verified against a real backend but **not rolled**. It needs a `cdk deploy GojoGoFargateStack` first (the review token is a new Secrets Manager secret), then the normal image deploy; runbook in the Phase 2b M6 section. Until it lands, GojoDelivery stays empty in prod.
2. **Admin-side create for Gojo Admin** — the small backend slice M6 now implies: `POST /v1/partner/admin/applications` (create on a merchant's behalf, owner id supplied) and an admin document-upload path, both on the existing token-guarded surface. Without it, creating a restaurant means editing the database by hand.
3. **Stripe + Connect checkout + a `payments` ledger** — needs your Stripe account and keys (user action) before anything can be built past the ledger schema. Three things now hang off it: the M3 conversation `ConversationContext` (kind/refId) for a marketplace sale, the M4 delivery order (placed with no payment step), and M6's partner, who is the payee.
4. **Order push notifications** — `delivery.OrderStatusChanged` is published on every transition and has no consumer; `notifications` now consumes both `messaging.MessageSent` and `partner.PartnerReviewed` the same way, so "your food is on the way" is a small, self-contained slice — and with M6 deployed there is finally a way to place a real order to test it.
5. **Search** — the `search` module doesn't exist at all yet, so this is "build search, then pick a backend". **Postgres full-text search** (`tsvector` + GIN, `pg_trgm` for typos) costs $0 and would be indistinguishable to a user at current volumes; **OpenSearch** is ~$25+/mo on top of ~$102–109 and earns it once one query has to rank people, posts, listings, restaurants and videos together. Recommendation on 2026-07-27: build the module on Postgres behind the same public API and the same event-driven indexing, so swapping later changes no interface. `ListingCreated` stays published either way.
6. **Listing-context polish** — carry the card onto the conversation-list row too (M3 only shows it inside the open thread), and fan out a live socket update when the card is refreshed on reuse (the seller currently learns it on next fetch).
7. **Merchant follow-ons** — opening hours, per-merchant order list (waits on Phase 3 `dispatch`, see M6), tag/category ordering, dish options and extras, and a map pin for the kitchen.
8. **Delivery follow-ons** — address autocomplete / map-pin picking, and live courier geo (that one waits for Phase 3 `dispatch`).

Also consider swapping the My World OTP to Twilio/Vonage Verify before going live (SMS provider options discussed 2026-07-23).
